<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B88B846A-865E-44BA-90E3-A8A3C6065169'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Put the following in order of size, **with the smallest first**.

$0.43, \quad \dfrac{3}{7}, \quad \dfrac{2}{5}, \quad 44\%$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

In this case, there are more fractions than percentages, so let's convert all the numbers into fractions.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.43,  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad \dfrac {2} {5}, \quad 44 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {43} {100},  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad \dfrac {2} {5}, \quad \dfrac {44} {100}
\end{aligned}
$

LCM for $100, 7, 5, 100 = 700$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {43 \times 7} {100 \times 7},  \quad \dfrac {3 \times 100} {7 \times 100}, \quad \dfrac {2 \times 140} {5 \times 140}, \quad \dfrac {44 \times 7} {100 \times 7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {301} {700},  \quad \dfrac {300} {700}, \quad \dfrac {280} {700}, \quad \dfrac {308} {700}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {280} {700},  \quad \dfrac {300} {700}, \quad \dfrac {301} {700}, \quad \dfrac {308} {700} \\\\ 
&= \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad 0.43, \quad 44 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {5},  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad 0.43, \quad 44 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 
<emptyline>
In this case, there are more fractions than percentages, so let's convert all the numbers into fractions.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.43,  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad \dfrac {2} {5}, \quad 44 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {43} {100},  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad \dfrac {2} {5}, \quad \dfrac {44} {100}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
LCM for $100, 7, 5, 100 = 700$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {43 \times 7} {100 \times 7},  \quad \dfrac {3 \times 100} {7 \times 100}, \quad \dfrac {2 \times 140} {5 \times 140}, \quad \dfrac {44 \times 7} {100 \times 7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {301} {700},  \quad \dfrac {300} {700}, \quad \dfrac {280} {700}, \quad \dfrac {308} {700}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's put them in ascending order now.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {280} {700},  \quad \dfrac {300} {700}, \quad \dfrac {301} {700}, \quad \dfrac {308} {700} \\\\ 
&= \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad \dfrac {3} {7}, \quad 0.43, \quad 44 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C8A77F0B-4C4D-421C-877B-72F489BBA0E6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Four lamp posts are in a straight line. The distance from each post to the next is $25 \ \text{m}$. 
What is the distance from the first post to the last?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Distance from the first post to the last} &= 25 + 25 + 25 \\\\
                                                 &= 75 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$75 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Distance from the first post to the last} &= 25 + 25 + 25 \\\\
                                                 &= 75 \ \text {m}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the highest common factor of $36$ and $18$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 36} &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36 \\\\
\text{Factors of 18} &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18 \\\\
\text{Highest common factors of 18 and 36} &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 36} &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18, 36 \\\\
\text{Factors of 18} &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 9, 12, 18 \\\\
\text{Highest common factors of 18 and 36} &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

$60$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9B2BAD76-821C-4206-AED5-F5F1A8A5545E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Ali has six times as much chocolate as Beth. Carl has twice as much chocolate as Beth. If Carl has $200 \ \text{g}$ of chocolate, how much chocolate does Ali have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the chocolate with Beth $= a$

Chocolate with Ali $= 6 a$

Chocolate with Carl $= 2 a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Chocolate with Beth} &= \dfrac {\text {Chocolate with Carl}} {2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac {200} {2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac {100 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac {100 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                          &= 100 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Chocolate with Ali}  &= 6 \times a \\\\
                           &= 6 \times 100 \\\\
                           &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$600 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the chocolate with Beth $= a$
<emptyline>
Chocolate with Ali $= 6 a$
<emptyline>
Chocolate with Carl $= 2 a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Chocolate with Beth} &= \dfrac {\text {Chocolate with Carl}} {2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac {200} {2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac {100 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                          &= \dfrac {100 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                          &= 100 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Chocolate with Ali}  &= 6 \times a \\\\
                           &= 6 \times 100 \\\\
                           &= 600 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$600 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DB9D1025-5B8E-4705-909E-7C92C3171975'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A right-angled triangle has the dimensions as shown. What is the area of the **unshaded** region?

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of triangle} = \dfrac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height}$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of unshaded region} &= \text {Area of whole triangle} - \text {Area of shaded region} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text {base1} \times \text {height1} - \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text {base2} \times \text {height2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 6 \times 3 - \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 3} {2} - \dfrac {4 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 3} {2} - \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} - \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                                &= 9 - 6 \\\\
                                &= 3 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3 \ \text {cm} ^ 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_triangle_expanded
<emptyline>
As per the diagram,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Area of unshaded region} &= \text {Area of whole triangle} - \text {Area of shaded region} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text {base1} \times \text {height1} - \dfrac {1} {2} \times \text {base2} \times \text {height2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 6 \times 3 - \dfrac {1} {2} \times 4 \times 3 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {6 \times 3} {2} - \dfrac {4 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 3} {2} - \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} - \dfrac {2 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
                                &= 9 - 6 \\\\
                                &= 3 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3 \ \text {cm} ^ 2$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7B55D3DF-EF43-4C91-9AFF-E371FEF2FC3D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find $5\%$ of $24$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5 \% \text{ of } 24 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5} {100} \times 24 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 24} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 4 \times 6} {4 \times 5 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 6} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 5 \times  5} \\\\
&= \dfrac  {6}{5} \\\\
&= 1 \dfrac  {1}{5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 \dfrac  {1}{5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5 \% \text{ of } 24 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5} {100} \times 24 \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 24} {100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5 \times 4 \times 6} {4 \times 5 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 6} {\cancel 4 \times \cancel 5 \times  5} \\\\
&= \dfrac  {6}{5} \\\\
&= 1 \dfrac  {1}{5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1 \dfrac  {1}{5}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What temperature is $12$ degrees colder than $-4 \degree \text{C}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= - 4 - 12 \\\\
&= - 16 \degree \text{C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$- 16 \degree \text{C}$

</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sarina has} &= -40 + 70 \\\\

\end{aligned}

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-FB0BA478-C8E7-46D4-B41D-FC926DEEE733'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows a rectangle. 

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The length of the rectangle is $2n + 1$. 

The width of the rectangle is $3n - 9$. 

Write down an expression for the perimeter of the rectangle. **Fully simplify your answer**.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the rectangle} &= 2 \times ((2 n + 1) + (3 n - 9)) \\\\
                                   &= 2 \times (2 n + 3n + 1 - 9) \\\\
                                   &= 2 \times (5 n - 8) \\\\
                                   &= 10 n - 16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$10 n - 16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter of the rectangle} &= 2 \times ((2 n + 1) + (3 n - 9)) \\\\
                                   &= 2 \times (2 n + 3n + 1 - 9) \\\\
                                   &= 2 \times (5 n - 8) \\\\
                                   &= 10 n - 16
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$10 n - 16$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many pupils are there in year $8$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of pupils in year 8 $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {4} \ \text{of} \ a &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4} \times a        &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {4}                 &= 30 \\\\
a                              &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                              &= 120
\end{aligned}
$

Number of pupils in year 8 $= 120$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$120$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of pupils in year 8 $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {4} \ \text{of} \ a &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {4} \times a        &= 30 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {4}                 &= 30 \\\\
a                              &= 30 \times 4 \\\\
a                              &= 120
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of pupils in year 8 $= 120$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many pupils chose green or blue?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the pie chart,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Pupils who chose green or blue} &= \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{of} \ 120 \\\\
                                      &=  \dfrac {120} {2} \\\\
                                      &=  \dfrac {60 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {60 \times \cancel 2} { \cancel 2} \\\\
                                      &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-F202EB77-1406-4EA6-951F-C009520818C9'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Jeff goes to the cinema. He buys a ticket for $\pounds 5.75$, popcorn for $\pounds 1.50$ and a drink for $76 \text{p}$. 
His return train fare is $\pounds 3.80$. If he leaves home with a $\pounds 20$ note how much change does he return with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Change Jeff will receive} &= \pounds 20 - \pounds 5.75 - \pounds 1.50 - 76 \text {p} - \pounds 3.80 \\\\
                                 &= \pounds 20 - \pounds 5.75 - \pounds 1.50 - \pounds \dfrac{76} {100} - \pounds 3.80 \\\\
                                 &=  20 - 5.75 - 1.50 -  0.76 - 3.80 \\\\
                                 &= 20 - 11.81 \\\\
                                 &= \pounds 8.19
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 8.19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Change Jeff will receive} &= \pounds 20 - \pounds 5.75 - \pounds 1.50 - 76 \text {p} - \pounds 3.80 \\\\
                                 &= \pounds 20 - \pounds 5.75 - \pounds 1.50 - \pounds \dfrac{76} {100} - \pounds 3.80 \\\\
                                 &=  20 - 5.75 - 1.50 -  0.76 - 3.80 \\\\
                                 &= 20 - 11.81 \\\\
                                 &= \pounds 8.19
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 8.19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$2x - 3 = 11$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
2x - 3  &= 11 \\\\
2x      &= 11 + 3 \\\\
2x      &= 14 \\\\
x       &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
x       &= \dfrac {2 \times 7} {2} \\\\
x       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 7} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x       &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
2x - 3  &= 11 \\\\
2x      &= 11 + 3 \\\\
2x      &= 14 \\\\
x       &= \dfrac {14} {2} \\\\
x       &= \dfrac {2 \times 7} {2} \\\\
x       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 7} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x       &= 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\dfrac{2}{3}x - 2 = 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {2} {3} x - 2  &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3} x      &= 4 + 2 \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3} x      &= 6 \\\\
x                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 6} {2} \\\\
x                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
x                     &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x                     &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
x                     &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {2} {3} x - 2  &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3} x      &= 4 + 2 \\\\
\dfrac {2} {3} x      &= 6 \\\\
x                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 6} {2} \\\\
x                     &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 3} {2} \\\\
x                     &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {2} \times 3} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x                     &= 3 \times 3 \\\\
x                     &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$3(x - 2) - 2(x - 3) = 4$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
3(x - 2) - 2(x - 3)  &= 4 \\\\
3x - 6 - 2x + 6      &= 4 \\\\
3x - 2x - 6 + 6      &= 4 \\\\
x                    &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
3(x - 2) - 2(x - 3)  &= 4 \\\\
3x - 6 - 2x + 6      &= 4 \\\\
3x - 2x - 6 + 6      &= 4 \\\\
x                    &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-B262AEAA-906F-455F-9F60-3F9875266B0B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Anna has $3$ brothers and $5$ sisters. Her brother Tom has $S$ sisters and $B$ brothers. What is the value of $S \times B$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
S \times B &= 6 \times 2 \\\\
           &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
S \times B &= 6 \times 2 \\\\
           &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DAF3531A-EB9D-4CFC-8D7B-E29EB5E1523E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Which fraction is half way between $\dfrac{3}{7}$ and $\dfrac{4}{7}$?

Give your answer in its simplest form.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \bigg( \dfrac {3} {7} + \dfrac {4} {7} \bigg) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \bigg( \dfrac {3 + 4} {7} \bigg) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \bigg( \dfrac {7} {7} \bigg) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 1 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \bigg( \dfrac {3} {7} + \dfrac {4} {7} \bigg) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \bigg( \dfrac {3 + 4} {7} \bigg) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \bigg( \dfrac {7} {7} \bigg) \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 1 \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7FE7F568-7B71-4112-B005-8AAA4EE29378'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$\pounds 50000$ in $\pounds 50$ notes weighs about $1.3 \ \text{Kg}$. How much, **in grams**, does one $\pounds 50$ note weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of notes}      &= \dfrac {50000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 \times 1000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 1000} {\cancel{50}} \\\\
                            &= 1000 \\\\
\text{Weight of a note}     &= \dfrac {1.3} { 1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1000} { 1000} \ \text {g} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} { 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0} } \\\\
                            &= 1.3 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.3 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of notes}      &= \dfrac {50000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {50 \times 1000} {50} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {\cancel{50} \times 1000} {\cancel{50}} \\\\
                            &= 1000 \\\\
\text{Weight of a note}     &= \dfrac {1.3} { 1000} \ \text{kg} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1000} { 1000} \ \text {g} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {1.3 \times 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0}} { 1 \cancel{0} \cancel{0} \cancel{0} } \\\\
                            &= 1.3 \ \text {g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.3 \ \text {g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

$x \degree$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Sum of angles in a triangle} = 180 \degree$


As the triangle is an isosceles triangle, the third angle is also $= x$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
x + 40 + x  &= 180 \\\\
2 x + 40    &= 180 \\\\
2 x         &= 180 - 40 \\\\
2 x         &= 140 \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {140} {2} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {70 \times 2} {2} \\\\
x           &= \dfrac {70 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
x           &= 70 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$70 \degree$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_angles_of_triangle
<emptyline>

x           &= 70 \degree
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\begin{aligned}
\text{P(a white square or a black circle)}     &= \dfrac {6 + 4} {18} \\\\

                                               &= \dfrac {2 \times 5} {2 \times 9} \\\\
                                               &= \dfrac {\cancel{2} \times 5} {\cancel{2} \times 9} \\\\
                                               &= \dfrac {5} {9}
\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1B75D1B9-5669-4840-977E-AF67A1065F16'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

To make porridge, Goldilocks mixes $3$ bags of oats with $1$ bag containing $20\%$ wheat bran and $80\%$ oats. 
All the bags have the same volume. What percentage of the porridge mix is wheat bran?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage} &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{20}{100} \times \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5 \times 4} \times 100  \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 }{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times 5}{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                  &= 5 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage} &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac{20}{100} \times \dfrac{1}{4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5 \times 4} \times 100  \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 }{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 5 \times 4} \times 100 \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {2 \times 2 \times 5 \times 5}{2 \times 2 \times 5} \\\\
                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times 5}{\cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                  &= 5 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$5 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-251FBF03-47A6-429C-803A-6682014D04F1'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

When a glass is full of water the total mass is $400 \ \text{g}$. 
When the glass is half full of water the total mass is $320 \ \text{g}$. Find the mass of the glass.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the mass of water $= w$

Let the mass of glass $= g$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
w + g                                              &= 400 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + g                                   &= 320 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + \dfrac{g \times 2}{1 \times 2}      &= 320 \\\\
w + \dfrac{2g}{2}                                  &= 320 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 320 \times 2 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 720
\end {aligned}
$

We have got 2 variables and we have 2 equations to solve them. Lets eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.

Subtract the first equation from the second to reduce it to one variable.

$
\begin {aligned}
w + 2g - w - g                &= 720 - 400 \\\\
g                             &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end {aligned}
$

Mass of glass $= 240 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of half water} &= 400 - 320 \\\\
                          &= 80 \\\\
\text{Mass of water}      &= 2 \times 80 \\\\
                          &= 160 \\\\
\text{Mass of glass}      &= \text{Total mass} - \text{Mass of water} \\\\
                          &= 400 -  160 \\\\
                          &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the mass of water $= w$
<emptyline>
Let the mass of glass $= g$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
w + g                                              &= 400 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + g                                   &= 320 \\\\
\dfrac{w}{2} + \dfrac{g \times 2}{1 \times 2}      &= 320 \\\\
w + \dfrac{2g}{2}                                  &= 320 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 320 \times 2 \\\\
w + 2g                                             &= 720
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We have got 2 variables and we have 2 equations to solve them. Lets eliminate to reduce it to just one variable.
<emptyline>
Subtract the first equation from the second to reduce it to one variable.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
w + 2g - w - g                &= 720 - 400 \\\\
g                             &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of glass $= 240 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Mass of half water} &= 400 - 320 \\\\
                          &= 80 \\\\
\text{Mass of water}      &= 2 \times 80 \\\\
                          &= 160 \\\\
\text{Mass of glass}      &= \text{Total mass} - \text{Mass of water} \\\\
                          &= 400 -  160 \\\\
                          &= 240 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The mean age of the five members of the `Macaroon $5$' boy band is $18$. 

A new member joins the band. His age is $24$. 
What is the new mean age of the group?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of ages}      &= 18 \times 5 \\\\
                        &= 90 \\\\
\text{New Mean}         &= \dfrac {90 + 24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {114} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 19} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 19} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$19$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Sum of ages}      &= 18 \times 5 \\\\
                        &= 90 \\\\
\text{New Mean}         &= \dfrac {90 + 24} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {114} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {6 \times 19} {6} \\\\
                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 6 \times 19} {\cancel 6} \\\\
                        &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$19$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find $4$ numbers which have a mean of $7$, a median of $8$ and a mode of $9$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the four numbers $= a1, a2, a3, a4$

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {a1 + a2 + a3 + a4} {4}          &= 7 \\\\
a1 + a2 + a3 + a4                       &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
a1 + a2 + a3 + a4                       &= 28
\end {aligned}
$

Lets arrange the numbers in ascending order.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Ordered numbers}          &= a1, \quad \maroonC
{a2}, \quad \maroonC
{a3}, \quad a4 \\\\
\dfrac {a2 + a3} {2}            &= 8 \\\\
a2 + a3                         &= 8 \times 2 \\\\
a2 + a3                         &= 16
\end {aligned}
$

Mode $= 9$

Atleast two numbers $= 9$

$a3 = 9$

$a4 = 9$

Lets use value of a3 in second equation to get the value of a2.

$
\begin {aligned}
a2 + a3                         &= 16 \\\\
a2                              &= 16 - a3 \\\\
a2                              &= 16 - 9 \\\\
a2                              &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

Lets use value of a2, a3 and a4 in first equation to get the value of a1.

$
\begin {aligned}
a1 + a2 + a3 + a4               &= 28 \\\\
a1                              &= 28 - a2 - a3 - a4 \\\\
a1                              &= 28 - 7 - 9 - 9 \\\\
a1                              &= 3
\end {aligned}
$

Required four numbers $= 3, 7, 9, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3, 7, 9, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the four numbers $= a1, a2, a3, a4$
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}

a1 + a2 + a3 + a4                       &= 28
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lets arrange the numbers in ascending order.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}

\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>

$

</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The mean weight of $5$ children is $45 \ \text{kg}$.

The mean weight of the lightest three children is $42 \ \text{kg}$ and the mean weight of the heaviest three children is $49 \ \text{kg}$. 

What is the median weight of the children in kg?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: If there is an odd number of numbers, the median is the middle number. If there is an even count of numbers, the median is the average of the middle two numbers.


ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


Let the weights of $5$ children $= w1, w2, w3, w4, w5$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {w1 + w2 + w3 + w4 + w5} {5}      &= 45 \\\\
w1 + w2 + w3 + w4 + w5                   &= 45 \times 5 \\\\
w1 + w2 + w3 + w4 + w5                   &= 225 \\\\
\dfrac {w1 + w2 + w3} {3}                &= 42 \\\\
w1 + w2 + w3                             &= 42 \times 3 \\\\
w1 + w2 + w3                             &= 126 \\\\
\dfrac {w3 + w4 + w5} {3}                &= 49 \\\\
w3 + w4 + w5                             &= 49 \times 3 \\\\
w3 + w4 + w5                             &= 147
\end {aligned}
$

Let's add second and third equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
w1 + w2 + w3 + w3 + w4 + w5             &= 126 + 147 \\\\
w1 + w2 + 2w3 + w4 + w5                 &= 273
\end{aligned}
$

Let's subtract first equation from fourth equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
w1 + w2 + 2w3 + w4 + w5 - (w1 + w2 + w3 + w4 + w5)      &= 273 - 225 \\\\
w1 - w1 + w2 - w2 + 2w3 - w3 + w4 - w4 + w5 - w5        &= 48 \\\\
w3                                                      &= 48 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Median weight of the children $= 48 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_median
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
Let the weights of $5$ children $= w1, w2, w3, w4, w5$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}

w3 + w4 + w5                             &= 49 \times 3 \\\\

\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
w1 + w2 + 2w3 + w4 + w5 - (w1 + w2 + w3 + w4 + w5)      &= 273 - 225 \\\\
w1 - w1 + w2 - w2 + 2w3 - w3 + w4 - w4 + w5 - w5        &= 48 \\\\
w3                                                      &= 48 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Median weight of the children $= 48 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-89E47B93-00E0-4CFB-BD8B-1C27D2333057'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A rectangle has a length of $50 \ \text{mm}$ and has the same area as a square with side $70 \ \text{mm}$.

Calculate the perimeter of the rectangle.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


Let width of the rectangle $= w$

Let side of the square $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
50 \times w             &= 70 \times 70 \\\\
50 \times w             &= 4900 \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {4900} {50} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {50 \times 98} {50} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 98} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
w                       &= 98 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}           &= 2 \times (50 + 98) \\\\
                            &= 2 \times 148 \\\\
                            &= 296 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$296 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_rectangle
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

50 \times w             &= 4900 \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {4900} {50} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {50 \times 98} {50} \\\\
w                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {50} \times 98} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
w                       &= 98 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's find the perimeter of the rectangle.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}           &= 2 \times (50 + 98) \\\\
                            &= 2 \times 148 \\\\
                            &= 296 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AA8ED085-BF3B-4E90-AC3B-C92BC01655FF'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A bag contains $49$ red balls and $1$ blue ball. How many red balls should be removed for the bag to contain $90\%$ red balls?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of red balls removed $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {49 - b} {50 - b}                                             &= \dfrac {90} {100} \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {90} {100} \times (50 - b) \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {90} {100} \times 50 - \dfrac {90} {100} \times b \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {45 \times 2 \times 50} {50 \times 2} - \dfrac {90 b} {100} \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {50}} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel {2}} - \dfrac {90 b} {100} \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= 45 - \dfrac {90 b} {100} \\\\
-b + \dfrac {90 b} {100}                                             &= 45 - 49 \\\\
-\dfrac {b \times 100} {1 \times 100} + \dfrac {90 b} {100}          &= -4 \\\\
-\dfrac {100 b + 90 b} {100}                                         &= -4 \\\\
-\dfrac {10 b} {100}                                                 &= -4 \\\\
\dfrac {10 b} {100}                                                  &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {1 \cancel {0} b} {10 \cancel {0}}                            &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {b} {10}                                                      &= 4 \\\\
b                                                                    &= 4 \times 10 \\\\
b                                                                    &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

Let the total number of balls after red balls removed $= a$

Number of blue balls,

$
\begin{aligned}
10 \% \ \text{of} \ a            &= 1 \\\\
\dfrac {10} {100} \times a   &= 1 \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {100} {10} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                            &= 10
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Red balls}           &= \text{Total balls} - \text {Blue balls} \\\\
                           &= 10 - 1 \\\\
                           &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Red balls removed}   &= 49 - 9 \\\\
                           &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of red balls removed $= b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {49 - b} {50 - b}                                             &= \dfrac {90} {100} \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {90} {100} \times (50 - b) \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {90} {100} \times 50 - \dfrac {90} {100} \times b \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {45 \times 2 \times 50} {50 \times 2} - \dfrac {90 b} {100} \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= \dfrac {45 \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {50}} {\cancel {50} \times \cancel {2}} - \dfrac {90 b} {100} \\\\
49 - b                                                               &= 45 - \dfrac {90 b} {100} \\\\
-b + \dfrac {90 b} {100}                                             &= 45 - 49 \\\\
-\dfrac {b \times 100} {1 \times 100} + \dfrac {90 b} {100}          &= -4 \\\\
-\dfrac {100 b + 90 b} {100}                                         &= -4 \\\\
-\dfrac {10 b} {100}                                                 &= -4 \\\\
\dfrac {10 b} {100}                                                  &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {1 \cancel {0} b} {10 \cancel {0}}                            &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {b} {10}                                                      &= 4 \\\\
b                                                                    &= 4 \times 10 \\\\
b                                                                    &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Alternate solution.
<emptyline>
Let the total number of balls after red balls removed $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of blue balls,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
10 \% \ \text{of} \ a            &= 1 \\\\
\dfrac {10} {100} \times a   &= 1 \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {100} {10} \\\\
a                            &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                            &= 10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Red balls}           &= \text{Total balls} - \text {Blue balls} \\\\
                           &= 10 - 1 \\\\
                           &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Red balls removed}   &= 49 - 9 \\\\
                           &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$40$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9C476DF3-1D21-4622-8A07-7B3EB467FF54'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A ball is dropped and bounces up to a height that is $75\%$ of the height from which it
was dropped. It then bounces again to a height that is $75\%$ of the previous height and 
so on. How many bounces does it make before it bounces to a height that is less than 
$25\%$ of the original height?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{let bounces} &= x \\\\
\text{let height} &= h \\\\
\text{new height} &= 0.75 \times h \\\\
0.25 \times h &= (0.75)^x \times h \\\\
x &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
